Commenting on the incident to Associated Press, China's Ministry of National Defence dismissed the claims, stating that China maintains a prudent and responsible approach to military exports.[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262] The [US–China Economic and Security Review Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States%E2%80%93China_Economic_and_Security_Review_Commission) also alleged a disinformation campaign by China, stating that fake social media accounts were used to propagate AI and video game images of supposed 'debris' from Indian aircraft.[^cite_note-263] According to [French Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Air_and_Space_Force) Chief General Jérôme Bellanger, he had seen evidence that confirms the loss of a Rafale during the conflict.[^cite_note-264]

On 15 June, Indian media outlets, including [India Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_Today) and [The Print](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ThePrint), reported that Dassault CEO [Éric Trappier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Trappier) had publicly disputed Pakistan's previous claim that its air force had shot down IAF Rafales during Operation Sindoor, calling the assertion "inaccurate".[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266] However Dassault denied these reports and issued a statement: "In response to certain press reports quoting Dassault Aviation Chairman & CEO, Eric Trappier, about the Sindoor operation, Dassault Aviation formally denies that Eric Trappier has made any operational or technical comments regarding the use of the Rafale in this operation."[^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269] On 7 July, Indian [Defence Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Secretary_\(India\)) RK Singh while speaking to [CNBC TV18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNBC_TV18) dismissed claims that IAF Rafales had been shot down by Pakistan during Operation Sindoor and emphasised that the Indian armed forces had complete operational autonomy during the conflict.[^cite_note-270]

In January 2026, *[The Times of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_India)* reported that during the 26 January 2026 Republic Day parade, the IAF flew BS022 - the same Rafale that Pakistan had claimed to have destroyed. Previously, aerospace journalist Alan Warnes and retired Lt Gen Kidwai had claimed that Rafales with identification codes - BS001, BS021, BS022, and BS027 as being allegedly destroyed, however India has not confirmed losses.[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] In June 2026, the IAF issued a tender to seek "bridge support" for all 36 of its initial batch of Rafales; a *The Times of India* article interpreted the tender as contradicting the claim that no Rafales were lost in the conflict.[^cite_note-273]

##### Additional IAF procurement

Main article: [Indian MRCA competition § Additional Rafale procurement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_MRCA_competition#Additional_Rafale_procurement)

The government-to-government (G2G) deal for 114 Rafale aircraft under the [Multi-Role Fighter Aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_MRCA_competition#MRFA) (MRFA) programme was first reported in April 2025.[^cite_note-274] By August, IAF was actively pursuing for the deal to meet its urgent requirements amid its depleting fighter strength and was expected to submit a document to the Ministry of Defence for the procurement programme soon for an approval. Rafale's existing infrastructure at air bases where the jet is deployed — [Hasimara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasimara_Air_Force_Station) and [Ambala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambala_Air_Force_Station) — can host at least one more squadron of the jet each.[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276] The proposal document, officially the [Statement of Case](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statement_of_case) (SoC), was received by the Ministry of Defence in early September and was being considered by the Finance Division, chaired by Secretary (Defence Finance).[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278] By 17 September, the deal had been moved to fast track procurement mode and was expected to be signed in the next year.[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280]

On 16 January 2026, the Defence Procurement Board chaired by the [Defence Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Secretary_\(India\)), [Rajesh Kumar Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajesh_Kumar_Singh_\(civil_servant\)), cleared the acquisition proposal worth [₹](490783.md)3.25 [lakh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakh) [crore](121420.md) (€30 billion).[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282] The deal includes 88 single-seat variants and 26 twin-seat trainer variants.[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-\:32-284] The in-service 36 IAF Rafales might also be upgraded to the F4 standard under the agreement.[^cite_note-285]

The deal was granted the Acceptance of Necessity (AoN) during a Defence Acquisition Council (DAC) meeting chaired by the [Defence Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Defence_\(India\)), [Rajnath Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajnath_Singh), on 12 February 2026.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] According to the proposal document cleared by DAC, 18 fighters are to be delivered in fly away condition from France; the remaining 96 are to be manufactured in India with an indigenous content of over 25%.[^cite_note-\:42-288] The fly away jets are not expected to arrive before 2032 as per an [India Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_Today) article on 18 February while the Indian assembled jets are also expected to arrive by the same time. Though Dassault will reportedly integrate [Astra Mark 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astra_\(missile\)) and the [Smart Anti-Airfield Weapon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smart_Anti-Airfield_Weapon) on Rafale, the IAF will still need to depend on the firm to integrate further weapons since the source code is not acquired.[^cite_note-289] In March 2026, Dassault CEO [Éric Trappier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Trappier) explained that the firm will establish a second Rafale assembly line at [Hyderabad](37534.md), if the order is placed, with a target production rate yet to be decided. On the other hand, the indigenisation of these fighters is expected to be 50% by component numbers.[^cite_note-290]

In April 2026, it was reported that the [request for proposal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Request_for_proposal) (RfP) will be issued to Dassault aviation by May. This will be followed by contract negotiations during which the government will ensure that indigenous weapons remain compatible with the aircraft. The transfer of the [interface control document](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interface_control_document) (ICD) will be insisted on by the Indian site during the negotiations. The plan is to “hardwire” ICD into the final contract.[^cite_note-\:42-288]

The Acquisition Wing of the Indian MoD issued the [Letter of Request](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Letter_of_Request) (LoR) in the last week of May. This marked the formal commencement of the acquisition project. The LoR is a formal government-to-government communication used to initiate defence procurement under the Intergovernmental Agreement (IGA) framework.[^cite_note-291] The response to the LoR, the detailed technical and commercial proposal, was submitted by France on 29 July.[^cite_note-\:62-292] The proposal is being examined by the Acquisition Wing and the Indian Air Force.[^cite_note-293][^cite_note-294] The proposal also includes a detailed partnership roadmap for the project — Dassault has proposed a joint [final assembly line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assembly_line) with [Tata Advanced Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Advanced_Systems); a partnership between Thales and [Mahindra Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahindra_Group) to collaborate on radar and avionics; [MBDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBDA) is likely to partner with [Larsen & Toubro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larsen_&_Toubro) for armaments; and Safran will partner with [Hindustan Aeronautics Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustan_Aeronautics_Limited) for the aero engine segment. The major requirement were met by the proposal which is still under detailed evaluation. If there is no major clarification required, the procedure will directly skip to cost and contract negotiations. Since the France's proposed pricing falls within the DAC cleared budget of ₹3.25 lakh crore, officials aims to conclude the contract within this financial year.[^cite_note-\:62-292]

#### Indian Navy

In June 2012, [Flight Global](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FlightGlobal) reported that the [Indian Navy](604522.md) was considering the purchase of Rafale Ms for [INS *Vikrant*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS_Vikrant_\(2013\)).[^cite_note-FM-295] Subsequently, Rafale also won the IAF's [MMRCA competition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_MRCA_competition).[^cite_note-296] In January 2016, the Indian government directed the Indian Navy to be briefed by Dassault on the Rafale M for its aircraft carriers, promoting logistics and spares commonalities between the Navy and IAF.[^cite_note-297] In December 2016, the Indian Navy rejected the naval variant of the [HAL Tejas](749967.md) due to it lacking enough [thrust-to-weight ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrust-to-weight_ratio) with full fuel and weapons load being overweight making it incapable of operating optimally from aircraft carriers. Hence, the Navy was expecting to import fighters from abroad to fulfil its requirements within six years. By this point, it had inducted 30 of the 45 [MiG-29K](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MiG-29K) that had been ordered while the expected procurement of 50 Tejas Naval aircraft stood cancelled.[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299]

On 17 January 2017, the Indian Navy released a [Request for Information](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Request_for_information) to procure 57 fighters under the *Multi-Role Carrier Borne Fighter* (MRCBF) programme to form the [fighter wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier_air_wing) of [INS *Vikrant*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS_Vikrant_\(2013\)). The requirements included day-night and all-weather operation capability, shipborne air defence, air-to-surface, buddy-buddy [aerial refuelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerial_refuelling), [reconnaissance](146911.md), [electronic warfare](82272.md) among others. Deliveries would span between three and six months of the contract's signing.[^cite_note-janes.com2-300][^cite_note-301] By June 2017, Dassault with their Rafale M,[^cite_note-302] [Boeing](18933266.md) with [F/A-18E/F](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F/A-18E/F), [Saab](26968.md) with the [Gripen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gripen) Maritime and [UAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Aircraft_Corporation) with [MiG-29K](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MiG-29K) had formally responded to the RFI.[^cite_note-303] Talks with Dassault and Boeing began by 2018 while the [Request for Proposal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Request_for_Proposal) was expected that same year under a [strategic partnership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategic_partnership) (SP) model. The Navy was finalising its specific Naval Air Staff Requirements (NASR).[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305]

By December 2020, the number of fighters for the MRCBF was reduced to 36 amid a proposal by India's [DRDO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DRDO) and [ADA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeronautical_Development_Agency) to develop the [HAL TEDBF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAL_TEDBF) indigenously. There were also plans to merge the MRCBF procurement with the IAF's MRFA.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307] That same month, [Boeing Defense, Space & Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_Defense,_Space_&_Security), in coordination with the [United States Navy](20518076.md), demonstrated the F/A-18's capability to operate from a [STOBAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/STOBAR) carrier at [Naval Air Station Patuxent River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naval_Air_Station_Patuxent_River) by conducting eight ski-jumps.[^cite_note-\:15-308][^cite_note-309]

On 6 January 2022, a 12-day demonstration of the Rafale M took place at the 283 metre-long shore-based test facility (SBTF) at [INS *Hansa*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS_Hansa), Goa. The deal would be in the Government-to-Government (G2G) mode instead of buying directly from the manufacturers. The quantity was further reduced to 26.[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] Between 20 May[^cite_note-313] and 15 June 2022,[^cite_note-314] two F/A-18E/F Block III completed "operational demonstration tests" in the same facility. Trials included multiple ski-jumps, roll-ins, fly-in-arrestments in various configurations including air-to-air, air-to-ground, and air-to-surface making the jet "compliant" with Indian Navy aircraft carriers.[^cite_note-\:15-308][^cite_note-315] By 7 December 2022, the Indian Navy headquarters submitted a report to the ministry of defence in which the Rafale was allegedly the front runner after having met all criteria. The Navy HQ had performed detailed analysis of the trial report prepared by the officials who had undertaken the previous trials.[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317]

On 13 July 2023, Defence Acquisition Council (DAC) of India granted the Acceptance of Necessity (AoN) for the procurement of 26 Rafale M F4 for the Indian Navy along with three additional [*Kalvari*-class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalvari-class_submarine_\(2015\)) submarines.[^cite_note-318] Senior representatives from the Navy, Defense Acquisition Wing, along with Dassault and [Thales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thales_Group), commenced negotiations on 30 May 2024.[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320] By late June 2024, the base price of Rafale was decided to be same as that of the IAF.[^cite_note-\:14-321] On 3 September 2024, the Defence Acquisition Council dropped the integration of [Uttam AESA radar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uttam_AESA_Radar) and other indigenous weapons like [Astra missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astra_\(missile\)) on the Rafale M due to high costs and an estimated delay of 8 years for design changes.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-\:9-236] By 29 September 2024, Dassault submitted its final price offer for the 26 aircraft to the Navy.[^cite_note-\:9-236][^cite_note-323] The squadron will be based at [INS *Dega*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS_Dega), [Visakhapatnam](238760.md) and will form the Carrier Air Group of *Vikrant*.[^cite_note-The_Times_of_India-324]

The deal, worth [₹](490783.md)63,887 [crore](121420.md) (equivalent to ₹680 billion or €6.2 billion in 2026),[^cite_note-325] included purchase of weapon systems like [Meteor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meteor_\(missile\)) ([air-to-air](320568.md)), [Exocet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exocet) ([anti-ship](140725.md)) and [SCALP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SCALP_missile) ([cruise missile](6590.md)) along with five-year performance-based logistics support and training programmes for crew training to operate and maintain the jets, associated ancillary equipment, simulator, spares and Indian Navy-specific design alterations. INS Vikrant will also be equipped for Rafale-M operations as part of the deal.[^cite_note-\:10-235][^cite_note-326] Design alterations for Indian Navy jets over those of IAF include [helmet mounted display](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helmet-mounted_display), low band frequency jammers, improved [radio altimeter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radar_altimeter) and very high frequency range decoys as well as software changes for air to sea mode, [electromagnetic interference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electromagnetic_interference) (EMI) and [electromagnetic compatibility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electromagnetic_compatibility) (EMC) among others.[^cite_note-\:14-321] By 3 February 2025, the price negotiations were completed.[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328]

The [Cabinet Committee on Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_Committee_on_Security) cleared the deal on 9 April 2025. The IN-specific Rafale Ms will be showcased by Dassault within 18 months and all aircraft will be delivered within 37 to 65 months after the contract's signing.[^cite_note-\:12-329][^cite_note-330] The deal was signed on 28 April 2025,[^cite_note-\:13-331] and comprised 22 single-seat carrier-compatible aircraft and four twin-seat aircraft that will be solely used from land bases.[^cite_note-\:13-331][^cite_note-332] The first four Rafale-Ms will be delivered by 2029.[^cite_note-333] The programme was officially launched on 19 June as an Indian defence delegation, led by Joint Secretary and Acquisition Manager (Maritime Systems) Dinesh Kumar, met with French defence officials, led by [Lt Gen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lieutenant-General_\(France\)) Gael Diaz De Tuesta, Director General of Armament, during the [Paris Air Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Air_Show).[^cite_note-334] The first Rafale Marine will be delivered in 2028.[^cite_note-335]

On 15 February 2026, a [La Tribune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Tribune) report indicated that an additional order for 31 Rafale Marines is being considered by the Indian Navy.[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] The first aircraft delivery is expected in August–September as of 23 March.[^cite_note-\:02-338]

### Greece

In August 2020, the government of Greece announced the acquisition of 18 Rafales.[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340] Initial reports stated that ten would be the new Rafale C variant in F3-R standard with eight older Rafale in F1 and F2 standard in use with the French Air and Space Force that would be given to Greece.[^cite_note-341]

In January 2021, the [Hellenic Parliament](2449826.md) ratified the agreement with Dassault for the purchase of six new built and 12 used F3-R aircraft formerly used by the Armée de l'Air at a total cost of €2.4 billion, including armaments and ground support.[^cite_note-342] The inter-governmental agreement was signed on 25 January 2021 by the Defense Ministers of Greece and France.[^cite_note-343] This was followed by an additional contract in March 2022 to buy the six additional Rafales, to be delivered from mid-2024.[^cite_note-344] The first aircraft, a Rafale B two-seater, was delivered on 21 July 2021.[^cite_note-345] On 19 January 2022, the first six Rafales landed at [Tanagra Air Base](50870569.md) where a welcoming ceremony was held.[^cite_note-346] The type officially entered service in September 2023.[^cite_note-347] In 2024, it was reported that the Greek government was looking to buy 6 to 12 more Rafales (as well as another Frigate) on the 80th Anniversary of D-Day. They also wanted to negotiate postponing some payments on previous arms deals to 2028–2030, and negotiate the transfer of 24 Mirage 2000-R that they wanted to discard as partial payment.[^cite_note-348] In April 2026, President Macron offered Greece to transfer all of its aging Mirage 2000-5 aircraft to Ukraine and in return, get a discount on an equivalent number of new Rafales.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350]

### Croatia



Croatian Rafale F3-R in [Zagreb Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zagreb_Airport)

The Republic of [Croatia](5573.md) received a proposal for twelve second-hand Rafales F3Rs in September 2020 in a tender to replace the [Croatian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Air_Force)'s [MiG-21s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MiG-21). The total package offered costs €1 billion (including weapon systems, spare parts, logistics and training), and competed with new F-16V Block 70, Israeli used F-16C/D Barak raised to ACE configuration, and [Saab Gripen](87577.md).[^cite_note-Globus-351] On 28 May 2021, Croatian Prime Minister [Andrej Plenković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Plenkovi%C4%87) announced the purchase of twelve used Rafales F3-R C/B on order, ten single-seater C F3-R and two two-seater Rafale B F3Rs.[^cite_note-Vlada_RH-352][^cite_note-353] The contract was signed on 25 November 2021.[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-flightglobal.com-355]

On 2 October 2023, Croatia received the first of twelve Rafales during a ceremony at [Mont-de-Marsan Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mont-de-Marsan_Air_Base).[^cite_note-McNeil-356][^cite_note-janes.com-357] The 12th and final aircraft was delivered on 25 April 2025.[^cite_note-index.hr-358]

On 8 December 2025 in France, Croatian Defence Minister, and the French Minister for Armed Forces signed a contract Letter of Intent for further upgrading 12 Rafale aircraft from the Rafale F3-R standard to enhanced capabilities of the Rafale F4 standard.[^cite_note-auto3-359]

### United Arab Emirates

In 2009, the [United Arab Emirates Air Force](10815770.md) was interested in an upgraded Rafale with more powerful engines and radar, and advanced air-to-air missiles.[^cite_note-360] In October 2011, Dassault was confident that a US\$10 billion deal for up to 60 Rafales would be signed.[^cite_note-361] However, Deputy Supreme Commander of the [Union Defence Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Defence_Force_\(UAE\)), [Mohammed bin Zayed Al Nahyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_bin_Zayed_Al_Nahyan), in November 2011 called the French offer "uncompetitive and unworkable";[^cite_note-362] In 2010, France allegedly asked the UAE to pay US\$2.6 billion of the total cost of Rafale upgrades.[^cite_note-363] Consequently, the UAE explored a purchase of the Eurofighter Typhoon[^cite_note-364] or the F/A-18E/F Super Hornet.[^cite_note-365] The newspaper *La Tribune* reported in February 2012, that the UAE was still considering the US\$10-billion deal for 60 Rafales. Interoperability among the Gulf air forces had renewed Qatari and Kuwaiti interest in the Rafale.[^cite_note-366] In January 2013, President Hollande stated that he would discuss the Rafale during an official visit to the UAE.[^cite_note-367] In December 2013, the UAE reportedly chose not to proceed with a deal for defence and security services, including the supply of Typhoons.[^cite_note-368]

In September 2014, it was reported that the UAE could acquire 40 Rafales in addition to upgrading its existing Mirage 2000s.[^cite_note-369] In November 2015, Reuters reported that Major General Ibrahim Nasser Al Alawi, commander of the UAE Air Force and Air Defence, had confirmed that the UAE was in final negotiations to purchase 60 Rafales.[^cite_note-370] In 2019 a series of Rafale F3-R trials were conducted at Al Dhafra Air Base in the UAE.[^cite_note-371] On 3 December 2021, Dassault announced that the UAE had signed an order for 80 Rafale F4 in a government-to-government deal,[^cite_note-372] which made the UAE the largest Rafale operator in the region and second to France.[^cite_note-373] The deal makes the United Arab Emirates Air Force the first user of the Rafale F4 standard outside France.[^cite_note-374]

On 29 January 2025, UAE received its first Rafale at a ceremony in Paris. The first aircraft would be used for testing and training in France, while deliveries for the rest of the fleet to commence in 2027.[^cite_note-ambafrance1-375][^cite_note-376]

On 4 April 2026, the UAE announced its withdrawal from the financing deal with France for Rafale F5 standard. The UAE was to have provided about €3.5 billion to the development costs. Although the order for 80 Rafales is to continue, the [French government](37707412.md) will have to fund the F5 development costs by itself. According to sources this is due to France's refusal to provide access for local companies to technology.[^cite_note-377]

### Indonesia



An Indonesian Rafale DI F4.1 in-flight, May 2026

In January 2020, the Indonesian government expressed interest in buying up to 48 Rafales to modernise the [Indonesian Air Force](8842734.md).[^cite_note-latribune-378][^cite_note-Latribun-379] In February 2021, Indonesia's Minister of Defense [Prabowo Subianto](2829084.md) announced that the purchase of 36 units, as part of a large procurement programme including [A330](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A330_MRTT) tankers and complementary American products, was planned and that funds had been secured for its finalization.[^cite_note-380] On 7 June 2021, Indonesia signed a letter of intent to buy 36 Rafales and associated weapons and support.

On 20 January 2022, Prabowo Subianto confirmed that Indonesia had completed the negotiation of the contract pending activation of the formal agreement by France. On 10 February 2022, Dassault stated that Indonesia had officially signed an order for 42 Rafale F4[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] consisting of 26 single-seat and 16 double-seat.[^cite_note-omnirafalediei-383] The first tranche for six Rafales came into force in September 2022. On 10 August 2023, Dassault announced that a contract covering a second tranche of 18 Rafales for Indonesia had come into force that day, bringing the total under contract to 24.[^cite_note-384] On 8 January 2024, Dassault Aviation disclosed that the third, and final tranche of 18 Rafales came into force, bringing the total aircraft ordered to 42.[^cite_note-385]

During the French President Emmanuel Macron's visit to Indonesia on 28 May 2025, both countries signed a Letter of Intent to purchase various military hardware, including an undisclosed number of additional Rafales.[^cite_note-386] *La Tribune* reported that Indonesia would order "a dozen" additional Rafales.[^cite_note-387] The French media further reported on 4 July that Indonesia may order up to 24 additional Rafales.[^cite_note-388]

The Indonesian Air Force sent four combat pilots and twelve technicians to France in early August 2025 to attend a training program for the Rafales.[^cite_note-389]

Indonesia formally received its first batch of three Rafales on 28 November 2025 in a ceremony at a Dassault facility in Bordeaux.[^cite_note-indoacceptance-390] The first Rafales will be based at [Roesmin Nurjadin Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roesmin_Nurjadin_Air_Force_Base) in [Pekanbaru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pekanbaru), [Riau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riau).[^cite_note-391] The Rafales arrived in Pekanbaru on 23 January 2026.[^cite_note-indodelivery1-392] Another three was delivered in May 2026. The initial six Rafales were formally accepted into service on 18 May.[^cite_note-indodelivery2-393]

### Future operators

#### Serbia

[President of Serbia](774382.md), [Aleksandar Vučić](13349267.md), stated on 24 December 2021 that Serbia is interested in buying new Rafales to strengthen the [Serbian Air Force and Air Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Air_Force_and_Air_Defence).[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395] On 8 April 2024, President Vučić announced the country's intention to purchase 12 Rafales, stating that "concrete agreements regarding the purchase of Rafale jets" had been made with French President Emmanuel Macron. Contract negotiations were completed in August 2024.[^cite_note-396] The contract for nine single-seater and three two-seater Rafales F3s is worth [€](9472.md)2.7 billion ([US\$](18717338.md)3 billion).[^cite_note-serbia_rafale-397]

### Potential operators

#### Brazil

On 30 October 2024, the Brazilian media reported that France's President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) during his trip to [Rio de Janeiro](25936.md) to participate the [2024 G20 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_G20_Rio_de_Janeiro_summit) will offer to the Brazilian President [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md) a package of armaments, including 24 Rafales to replace the older [AMX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AMX_International_AMX) as the [Brazilian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Air_Force) is in a selection process of a replacement for their preferred attack jet.[^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400]

#### Iraq

In November 2020, Iraqi Defence Minister [Jumaa Anad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumaa_Anad) stated that [Iraq](7515928.md) plans to buy Rafales for the [Iraqi Air Force](1817852.md).[^cite_note-401] In February 2022, Iraq reportedly intends to acquire 14 Rafale F4s, payable in [crude oil](23195.md).[^cite_note-402] However, as of October 2024, it was still weighing its options with reports that France will not be selling the [Meteor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meteor_\(missile\)) AAM to Iraq if an order was placed.[^cite_note-403] Iraq and France are in negotiations as of 2026.[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406]

#### Malaysia

The Rafale was a contender for the replacement of the [Royal Malaysian Air Force](1137609.md)'s (RMAF) [Mikoyan MiG-29s](185653.md), with a requirement to equip three squadrons with 36 to 40 fighters with an estimated budget of RM6 billion to RM8 billion (US\$1.84 billion to US\$2.46 billion). Other competitors were the Eurofighter Typhoon, Boeing F/A-18/F Super Hornet and Saab JAS 39 Gripen.[^cite_note-407] In July 2017, acquisition efforts were suspended with the RMAF looking instead to buy new maritime patrol aircraft and advanced trainers with light attack capabilities to confront the growing threat of Islamist militants in the Southeast Asian region.[^cite_note-408]

#### Peru

In July 2024, it was reported that Peru as part of a revitalization program for the combat sections of the [Peruvian Air Force](1040948.md) (FAP) was considering the Rafale as one of the contenders for a recently launched contract for fighters.[^cite_note-409] General Carlos Enrique Chávez Cateriano, the commanding general of the FAP, announced on 8 July 2024 that a competition had been launched and the Rafale was one of two leading contenders, with the other leading contender being the [KAI KF-21 Boramae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAI_KF-21_Boramae).[^cite_note-410]

#### Saudi Arabia

In February 2022, *La Tribune* reported that [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) is interested in the Rafale,[^cite_note-411] then reported in December 2022 that Saudi Arabia would need between 100 and 200 fighters.[^cite_note-412] In October 2023, Saudi Arabian authorities officially asked the French company Dassault Aviation to send a quote and a proposed delivery schedule for 54 Rafale F4 combat aircraft.[^cite_note-413]

#### Ukraine

On 17 November 2025, the presidents of Ukraine and France signed a letter of intent on the purchase of up to 100 Rafale jets over a 10–year period.[^cite_note-414] No schedule was given on the first delivery, with French officials citing a 3-year pilot training timeline.[^cite_note-415] On 14 July 2026, Ukraine and France signed an agreement for the first tranche order of 16 Rafale, financed by a European Union loan facility. Training for Ukrainian personnel in France could start as early as 2026, and Ukraine would receive the first four aircraft shortly after they finished training.[^cite_note-416]

### Failed bids

The Rafale has been marketed for export to various countries. Various commentators and industry sources have highlighted the high cost of the aircraft as detrimental to the Rafale's sales prospects. Its acquisition cost is roughly US\$100 million (2010),[^cite_note-417] while its operational cost hovers around US\$16,500 (2012) for every flight-hour.[^cite_note-Flight_hour_costs-418] The Saab JAS 39 Gripen, in comparison, costs only US\$4,700 per flight-hour to operate.[^cite_note-Flight_hour_costs-418] According to a 2009 article by the Institute for Defense Studies and Analysis, unlike the American government and its relationship with Boeing and Lockheed Martin, the lack of communication between the French government and Dassault has hampered a worldwide cooperative sales effort, as demonstrated by the case with Morocco in 2007.[^cite_note-419]

#### Bangladesh

In March 2020, *[La Tribune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Tribune)* reported that France's Minister of the Armed Forces, Florence Parly, promoted the Rafale's performance to Bangladeshi Prime Minister [Sheikh Hasina](623439.md), who is also Minister of Defense.[^cite_note-420][^cite_note-421] As of 2025, Bangladesh was in the process of acquiring the Eurofighter Typhoon.[^cite_note-422]

#### Belgium

Main article: [Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II procurement § Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II_procurement#Belgium)

In 2009, Belgium suggested that they may buy F-35s in the 2020s to replace Belgium's 34 F-16A/B MLU fleet.[^cite_note-423] An article published in Belgian newspaper [L'Avenir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'Avenir_\(Belgian_newspaper\)) on 19 April 2015 speculated that, if the nuclear strike role via Belgium's [Nuclear sharing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_sharing) policy were retained in the request for proposals, Belgium would be almost forced to buy the F-35 as to maintain this role.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425] Belgium officially launched its F-16 replacement program in March 2017, issuing requests for proposals to three European and two US manufacturers: [Boeing Defense, Space & Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_Defense,_Space_&_Security), [Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin), Dassault, [Eurofighter GmbH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurofighter_GmbH) and [Saab Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saab_Group), offering the F/A-18E/F Super Hornet, [F-35 Lightning II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35_Lightning_II), Rafale, Eurofighter Typhoon and Saab JAS 39 Gripen respectively.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427] On 25 October 2018, Belgium officially selected the offer for 34 F-35As; government officials stated that it had come down to price, and that "The offer from the Americans was the best in all seven evaluation criteria". The total purchasing price for the aircraft and support until 2030 totaled €4 billion, €600 million cheaper than the budgeted €4.6 billion.[^cite_note-BelgiumReuters-428][^cite_note-BelgiumDefenseNews-429] In April 2020, the first F-35 contract was signed, with deliveries to begin in 2023.[^cite_note-430]

#### Brazil

Main article: [Saab JAS 39 Gripen § Brazil](87577.md#Brazil)

In June 2008, the [Brazilian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Air_Force) issued a request for information on the following aircraft: F/A-18E/F Super Hornet, F-16 Fighting Falcon, Rafale, [Su-35](314292.md), [Gripen NG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gripen_NG) and Eurofighter Typhoon.[^cite_note-431] In October 2008, the service selected three finalists for F-X2 – Dassault Rafale, Gripen NG and Boeing F/A-18E/F.[^cite_note-432] On 5 January 2010, media reports stated that the final evaluation report by the Brazilian Air Force placed the Gripen ahead of the other two contenders based on unit and operating costs.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434] In February 2011, Brazilian President [Dilma Rousseff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilma_Rousseff) had reportedly decided in favour of the F/A-18.[^cite_note-435] After [Edward Snowden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Snowden)'s revelation that the NSA had been intercepting Rouseff's private communications, and her ensuing fury, the Brazilian government selected the Gripen NG in December 2013 in a US\$5 billion deal to equip the air force.[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437]

#### Canada

Main article: [Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II Canadian procurement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II_Canadian_procurement)

The Rafale was amongst various fighters proposed to replace the [Royal Canadian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Canadian_Air_Force)'s [McDonnell Douglas CF-18 Hornet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonnell_Douglas_CF-18_Hornet).[^cite_note-438] In 2005, a report compiled by Canada's Department of Defence reviewing aircraft noted concerns over the Rafale's interoperability with US forces; Dassault had also been unable to confirm engine performance during cold weather conditions.[^cite_note-439] In July 2010, the Canadian government announced the F-35 as the CF-18's replacement; the nation was already a partner in the [Joint Strike Fighter program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Strike_Fighter_program) since 1997 and a Tier 3 partner for the F-35 since 2002.[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441] In December 2012, the Canadian government announced that the F-35 buy had been abandoned due to cost rises and that a fresh procurement process would begin.[^cite_note-442] In January 2013, Dassault responded to Canada's request for information.[^cite_note-443] Various aircraft were considered, including the F-35.[^cite_note-444] In January 2014, Dassault offered a contract with full technology transfer, allowing Canada to perform its own support and upgrades, thereby lowering long-term service costs.[^cite_note-445][^cite_note-446] In November 2018, Dassault withdrew from the competition, reportedly over interoperability and intelligence sharing requirements, particularly with the US, complicated by France's lack of involvement in the [Five Eyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Eyes) intelligence-sharing group.[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448]

#### Colombia

In June 2022, *La Tribune* reported Dassault made an offer for 15 fighters and 9 in option for the [Colombian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Air_Force). Colombia was interested in used ones, but France denied, taking into consideration it already sold 24 jets to Croatia and Greece.[^cite_note-449] On 21 December 2022, the Colombian government announced that they had shortlisted the Rafale for a potential 16 aircraft order to replace their aging [Kfir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IAI_Kfir).[^cite_note-450] Nevertheless, on 3 January 2023, Colombia and Dassault explained they could not come to an agreement, mainly because of the high price-tag of the planes. On 1 April, Colombia issued a new RFP for new planes, with the Rafale, the Gripen and the F-16 as favorites.[^cite_note-451] On 3 April 2025, Colombia announced it had selected the [Gripen E/F](87577.md) to replace its [Kfir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IAI_Kfir) fleet in a deal expected to include 16 to 24 units.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453]

#### Finland

Main article: [Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II procurement § Finland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II_procurement#Finland)

In June 2015, a working group set up by the Finnish MoD proposed starting the [HX Fighter Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HX_Fighter_Program) to replace the [Finnish Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finnish_Air_Force)'s current fleet of [F/A-18 Hornets](45998.md). The group recognises five potential types: Boeing F/A-18E/F Super Hornet, Dassault Rafale, Eurofighter Typhoon, Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II and Saab JAS 39 Gripen E/F.[^cite_note-454] In December 2015, the Finnish MoD informed Great Britain, France, Sweden and the US informing them of the launch of the HX Fighter Program to replace the Hornet fleet, which will be decommissioned by 2025, with multi-role fighters; the Rafale is mentioned as a potential fighter.[^cite_note-455] The request for information was sent in early 2016; five responses were received in November 2016.[^cite_note-456] In December 2021, the Finnish newspaper Iltalehti reported that several foreign and security policy sources had confirmed the Finnish Defense Forces' recommendation of the F-35 as Finland's next fighter due to its "capability and expected long lifespan".[^cite_note-457][^cite_note-458]

#### Kuwait

In February 2009, French President [Nicolas Sarkozy](557007.md) announced that Kuwait was considering buying up to 28 Rafales.[^cite_note-459] In October 2009, during a visit to Paris, the Kuwaiti Defence Minister expressed interest in the Rafale and said that he was awaiting Dassault's terms.[^cite_note-460] Islamist lawmakers in the Kuwaiti national assembly threatened to block such a purchase, accusing the Defence Minister of lack of transparency and being manipulated by business interests.[^cite_note-461] In January 2012, the French Defence Minister said that both Kuwait and Qatar were waiting to see if the UAE first purchased the Rafale and that Kuwait would look to buy 18–22 Rafales.[^cite_note-462] However, on 11 September 2015, Eurofighter announced that an agreement had been reached with Kuwait to buy 28 Typhoons.[^cite_note-kuwait-463][^cite_note-464]

#### Libya

In January 2007, the French newspaper *Journal du Dimanche* reported that Libya sought 13 to 18 Rafales "in a deal worth as much as US\$3.24 billion".[^cite_note-465] In December 2007, [Saif al-Islam Gaddafi](1196902.md) declared Libya's interest in the Rafale,[^cite_note-466] but no order was placed. French Rafales later attacked targets in Libya as part of the international [military intervention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_intervention) during the [2011 Libyan civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Libyan_civil_war).[^cite_note-467]

#### Morocco

In late 2007, *La Tribune* reported that a prospective US\$2.85 billion sale to Morocco had fallen through, the government selecting the F-16C/D instead.[^cite_note-468][^cite_note-Jarry_Emmanuel-469] While French Defense Minister [Hervé Morin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herv%C3%A9_Morin) labelled it as overly sophisticated and too costly, defense analysts have said that miscalculations of the DGA's offer price and hesitations over financing were detrimental to the negotiations.[^cite_note-Jarry_Emmanuel-469][^cite_note-470]

#### Oman

In February 2009, France offered Rafales to Oman to replace its ageing fleet of SEPECAT Jaguars.[^cite_note-471] In December 2012, Oman placed an order for 12 Typhoons.[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-Oman_typhoon-473]

#### Singapore

Main article: [Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II procurement § Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II_procurement#Singapore)

In 2005, the [Republic of Singapore Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Singapore_Air_Force) launched its Next Generation Fighter (NGF) programme to replace its ageing [A-4SU Super Skyhawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ST_Aerospace_A-4SU_Super_Skyhawk). Several options were considered and the [Defence Science & Technology Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Science_&_Technology_Agency) (DSTA) conducted a detailed technical assessment, simulations and other tests to determine the final selection. This reduced the list of competitors to the Rafale and the [F-15SG Strike Eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-15E_Strike_Eagle). In December 2005, Singapore ordered 12 F-15SGs.[^cite_note-474] According to *Defense Industry Daily*, key reasons for the selection were that, despite the Rafale's superior aerodynamics, it had insufficient range, weapons, and sensor integration.[^cite_note-475]

#### South Korea

In 2002, the [Republic of Korea Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Korea_Air_Force) chose the F-15K Slam Eagle over the Dassault Rafale, Eurofighter Typhoon and Sukhoi Su-35 for its 40 aircraft [F-X Phase 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-X_fighter_program#F-X_Phase_1) fighter competition.[^cite_note-476]

#### Switzerland

Main article: [Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II procurement § Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II_procurement#Switzerland)

In February 2007, Switzerland was reportedly considering the Rafale and other fighters to replace its ageing [Northrop F-5 Tiger IIs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_F-5).[^cite_note-477] A one-month evaluation started in October 2008 at Emmen Airforce Base, consisting of approximately 30 evaluation flights; the Rafale, along with the JAS 39 Gripen and the Typhoon, were evaluated.[^cite_note-478] Although a leaked [Swiss Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swiss_Air_Force) evaluation report revealed that the Rafale won the competition on technical grounds,[^cite_note-480] on 30 November 2011, the Swiss Federal Council announced plans to buy 22 Gripen NGs due to its lower acquisition and maintenance costs.[^cite_note-481] Due to [a referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Swiss_referendums), this purchase never happened.

In March 2018, Swiss officials named contenders in its Air 2030 program: The Rafale, Saab Gripen, Eurofighter Typhoon, Boeing F/A-18E/F Super Hornet and Lockheed Martin F-35.[^cite_note-482][^cite_note-483] In October 2018, the Swiss Air Force was reportedly limited to buying a single-engine fighter for budgetary reasons.[^cite_note-484] In May 2019, the Rafale performed demonstration flights at [Payerne Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Payerne_Air_Base) for comparison against other bids.[^cite_note-485] On 30 June 2021, the [Swiss Federal Council](218299.md) proposed to [Parliament](1797312.md) the acquisition of 36 F-35As[^cite_note-486][^cite_note-487] at a cost of up to 6 billion [Swiss francs](361362.md) (US\$6.5 billion), citing the aircraft's cost- and combat-effectiveness.[^cite_note-488] However, it was later confirmed that the costs are capped for a period of just 10 years.[^cite_note-489] The Liberal Greens have promised to examine the F-35's environmental impact.[^cite_note-490] The Swiss anti-military group [GSoA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GSoA) intended to contest the purchase in another national referendum supported by the [Green Party of Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Party_of_Switzerland) and the [Social Democratic Party of Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Democratic_Party_of_Switzerland) (which previously managed to block the Gripen).[^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492] In August 2022, they registered the initiative, with 120,000 people having signed in less than a year (with 100,000 required).[^cite_note-493]

On 15 September 2022, the Swiss National council gave the Federal council permission to sign the purchase deal, with a time limit for signing of March 2023.[^cite_note-494] The deal to buy 36 F-35A was signed on 19 September 2022, with deliveries to commence in 2027 and conclude by 2030, bypassing the popular initiative.[^cite_note-495][^cite_note-496]

#### Uzbekistan

On 26 November 2023, French President Emmanuel Macron offered Rafale to both Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan governments, according to *La Tribune.*[^cite_note-497] *Scramble* reported that Uzbekistan is interested in buying 24 Rafales, citing the source in France government.[^cite_note-498] Uzbekistan instead placed an order for J-10CEs in 2025, and started receiving them that year.[^cite_note-499][^cite_note-500]

## Variants



Rafale B/C and M

### French variants

Rafale A

Technology demonstrator, first flew in 1986.

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200292-7]

Rafale D

Dassault used this designation (D for *discret*) in the early 1990s to emphasise the new semi-stealthy design features.

[^cite_note-Gaines_p80-44]

Rafale B

Two-seater version for the French Air and Space Force.

[^cite_note-Eden_p169-36]

Rafale C

Same as Rafale B but single-seat version for the French Air and Space Force.

[^cite_note-Eden_p169-36]

Rafale M

Similar to Rafale C, but with modifications to allow operations from [CATOBAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CATOBAR) – equipped aircraft carriers. For carrier operations, the M model has a strengthened airframe, longer nose gear leg to provide a more nose-up attitude, larger [tailhook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tailhook) between the engines, and a built-in boarding ladder. Consequently, the Rafale M weighs about 500 kg (1,100 lb) more than the Rafale C.

[^cite_note-Eden_p169-36]

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200294–95-40]

&#x20;It is the only non-US fighter type cleared to operate from the decks of US carriers, using catapults and their [arresting gear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arresting_gear), as demonstrated in 2008 when six Rafales from *Flottille 12F* integrated into the [USS *Theodore Roosevelt*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Theodore_Roosevelt_\(CVN-71\)) Carrier Air Wing interoperability exercise.

[^cite_note-SAFRAN-RafaleUSS-501]

Rafale N

Originally called the Rafale BM, was a planned missile-only two-seater version for the *Aéronavale*.

[^cite_note-MacKenzie_p36-139]

&#x20;Budgetary constraints have been cited as grounds for its cancellation.

[^cite_note-502]

Rafale R

Proposed reconnaissance-oriented variant.

[^cite_note-richardson_114-5-85]

### Export variants

Rafale DM

Two-seater version for the Egyptian Air Force.

[^cite_note-503]

Rafale EM

Single-seat version for the Egyptian Air Force.

[^cite_note-504]

Rafale DH

Two-seater version for the Indian Air Force.

[^cite_note-auto2-505]

Rafale EH

Single-seat version for the Indian Air Force.

[^cite_note-auto1-506]

Rafale MH

Single-seater version for the Indian Navy; customized carrier-capable variant of the Rafale M, adapted for STOBAR and Indian weapon systems.

[^cite_note-507]

Rafale DQ

Two-seater version for the Qatar Emiri Air Force.

[^cite_note-shephard-508]

Rafale EQ

Single-seat version for the Qatar Emiri Air Force.

[^cite_note-shephard-508]

Rafale DG

Two-seater version for the Hellenic Air Force.

[^cite_note-\:1-509]

Rafale EG

Single-seat version for the Hellenic Air Force.

[^cite_note-\:4-510]

Rafale DI

Two-seater version for the Indonesian Air Force.

[^cite_note-omnirafalediei-383]

Rafale EI

Single-seat version for the Indonesian Air Force.

[^cite_note-omnirafalediei-383]

 [France](5843419.md)

In 2025 France informed that it plan to order a total of 286 Rafale by 2035 following the budget plan for 2026, with an additional order of up to 61 units. The previous orders was of 234 units. This order was made to reach and maintain the total number of Rafale to 225 active units by 2035, since France sold to Croatia (12) and Greece (12) from its own stock and lost 2 units in the 2024 collision crash and to replace older models that entered service in 1999.

[^cite_note-513]

[^cite_note-DID_Rafale-93]

&#x20;As of 2026, 189 units were delivered to the French Armed Forces, with 12 units sold to Greece and another 12 to Croatia, France currently operates around 165 Rafale. 124 in the Air and Space Force and 41 in the Navy.

[^cite_note-514]

&#x20;Once the deliveries of 286 units completed, France is expected to field 225 units. 185 for the Air and Space Force and 40 for the Navy. Option for up to 30 additional units to complement the 225 was mentioned by the Minister of the Defense in early 2025. Potentially raising the total fleet to 255 units.

  [^cite_note-546]

## Notable accidents

- On 6 December 2007, a French Air Force twin-seat Rafale crashed during a training flight. The pilot, who suffered from [spatial disorientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spatial_disorientation), died in the accident.

  [^cite_note-550]

- On 24 September 2009, after unarmed test flights, two French Navy Rafales returning to the aircraft carrier [*Charles de Gaulle*](639127.md), collided in mid-air about 30 kilometres (19 mi) from the town of [Perpignan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perpignan) in southwest France. One test pilot, identified as François Duflot, died in the accident, while the other was rescued.

  [^cite_note-551]

- On 28 November 2010, a Rafale from the carrier *Charles de Gaulle* crashed in the [Arabian Sea](2747.md). This aircraft was supporting Allied operations in Afghanistan. The pilot ejected safely and was rescued by a rescue helicopter from the carrier. Later reports said the engines stopped after being starved of fuel due to [confusion by the pilot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilot_error) in switching fuel tanks.

  [^cite_note-552]

- On 2 July 2012, during a joint exercise, a Rafale from the carrier *Charles de Gaulle* plunged into the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md). The pilot ejected safely and was recovered by an American search and rescue helicopter from the carrier [USS *Dwight D. Eisenhower*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Dwight_D._Eisenhower).

  [^cite_note-553]

- On 14 August 2024, two French Rafale B two-seaters collided over [Colombey-les-Belles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombey-les-Belles). While one pilot had ejected before crashing into the ground, the trainee and instructor of the second aircraft were reported missing. Both aircraft were based in [Saint-Dizier – Robinson Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint-Dizier_%E2%80%93_Robinson_Air_Base). By 15 August, the death of the missing pilots was announced.

  [^cite_note-554]

  [^cite_note-555]

**General characteristics**

- **Crew:** 1 or 2

- **Length:** 15.27 m (50 ft 1 in)

      - [ASN4G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASN4G) hypersonic nuclear missile (in development)
